Val Finds His Family at Crown Works

Established Val discovers his father and sister among the drugged prisoners in Crown Works Installation Seven's cell blocks on 24 Pollant 301.

Val Finds His Family at Crown Works

Quick Reference

In-world Date: 24 Pollant 301 Bloom

Era: The Acanthine

Location: Crown Works Installation Seven

Participants: Val

Session: Session 12

What Happened

Moving through the western cell wing, Val recognized it as the place his family is imprisoned. He broke into a guard's chest and found a drawing by his little sister reading 'I love you, daddy,' locked away among confiscated belongings. Fleeing through the cell blocks of Crown Works Installation Seven after the fight with Roots Arcanist Quill, Val found his father and sister among the drugged prisoners, their eyes glassy and obedient. With the main exit blocked and the building on fire, Dez and Val smashed a hole through a mortared stone wall and led them out into the night with the rest of the freed prisoners. Their names were not established. Exploring the western cell wing, Val recognized the layout and confirmed it as the block where his family is held. In a guard's locked chest he and Calanthe found a drawing made by his younger sister, labeled 'I love you, daddy,' depicting a family of four. The Crown had confiscated it and stored it with the guard's effects. Val came upon Cassimir and Maret Voss, who presented the encounter as an escorted audit. Val persuaded Cassimir, his best friend, to let him join, drawing him into the events that followed below. After climbing out of the vault, the party fled into Crown Works's cell blocks while the entrance burned. Rows of prisoners sat awake but drugged into unresponsive obedience; unlocking the doors did nothing. Val searched and found his father and sister among them, glassy-eyed and unable to recognize him, but following his lead when prompted. Dez and Val breached a mortared stone wall together (combined check 54, using a thunder-and-chromatic orb alongside a weapon strike), and Dez used Control Flames on a candle flame to guide the group through the gap. The session ended as the party led the prisoners out through the breach.

At dawn Val carried his still-drugged sister and father to his mother's home in the Blackthorn slums. Her sanity and hope returned the moment she saw her children. Val's mother directed the group to a conscription-victim support group founded by the late Lady of Blackthorn, wife of Lord Corvus Blackthorn and mother of Cassimir and Theron Blackthorn. Valerius Found Imprisoned at Crown Works

Developing The party discovers Valerius held in an abjuration cage inside Crown Works Installation Seven, powered by something beneath the facility.

Valerius Found Imprisoned at Crown Works

Quick Reference

In-world Date: 24 Pollant 301 Bloom

Era: The Acanthine

Location: Crown Works Installation Seven

Participants: Kirill Amaterasu, Cleke, Dez, Valerius

Session: Session 12

What Happened

A massive cage hung over the central chasm. Inside, a man sat cross-legged; he opened glowing eyes and called the party 'little lambs.' The shard of the God of Second Chances on Cleke's bow flared green at his presence. Dez later confirmed through Nal'Szomn in The Dreamcatcher that the prisoner is Valerius. Nal'Szomn warned that releasing him could cost lives. Kirill Amaterasu argued the Crown held Valerius to drain his divinity at the 26 Pollant demonstration and pushed for his release. After crossing the central chasm, Dez and Cleke reached a massive cage suspended over it. When Dez tapped the bars, the prisoner opened glowing eyes and called them 'little lambs.' The God of Second Chances shard on Cleke's bow shone and the cage flared green. Dez covered the shard, but light bled through his fingers. That night Nal'Szomn identified the prisoner as Valerius, the Warmind of strategy and war, guessed the Queen arranged his imprisonment, and warned that freeing him could bring casualties depending on his disposition toward those who freed him. The party now knows a living god is held inside Crown Works. Freeing him is possible but not safe: Nal'Szomn placed casualties as a likely outcome if Valerius proves unfriendly to whoever springs him. The cage's green flare suggests the shard network and Valerius are connected in some way not yet understood. Kirill argued that the Crown planned to drain Valerius's divinity at the coming demonstration and pressed the party to free him; Dez resisted. Cleke cast Detect Magic on the cage and found a powerful abjuration ward built to hold a god. Valerius confirmed that something beneath the cage supplied the power, though he did not know its nature or location.

Consequences

Superseded the same night: during the escape, the party freed Valerius after the theft of the Aether Core killed the cage's ward. See Valerius Freed at Crown Works. (Table correction, recorded 2026-07-12.)

Valerius Freed at Crown Works

Quick Reference

Era: The Acanthine

Location: Crown Works Installation Seven

What Happened

During the party's escape from the burning Crown Works Installation Seven, the abjuration ward on Valerius's cage failed: the hidden power source beneath the cage, which Valerius himself could not locate, turned out to be the Aether Core, and Sable's crew had just pried it from its pylons (Theft of the Aether Core). With the ward dead, the party opened the cage and freed the Warmind of strategy and war. He left on his own path rather than staying with the party; his whereabouts and intentions are unknown.

Recorded as a table correction on 2026-07-12; the original Session 12 digest mistakenly left Valerius caged.

  • Sable holds the core and wants the god; her ambition to consume him now requires finding him first.
  • Whoever wants a god drained, the Crown or Sable, must now hunt him down; how quickly the Crown organizes that hunt is not yet established.

Verbena Unmasked as a Queen's Thorn

Developing Listening through the East Wing grill as Verbena lectures Cattelya on how the Wilt came up the water stairs, Calanthe recognizes the Magister's grey brass-cornered ledger-book as the kind Quill carried at Crown Works Installation Seven, proving Verbena is a Queen's Thorn.

Verbena Unmasked as a Queen's Thorn

Quick Reference

In-world Date: 2 Caducan 301 Bloom

Era: The Acanthine

Location: Potentilla Manor

Participants: Calanthe Orchidaceae, Cleke, Dez, Kirill Amaterasu, Magister Ilex Verbena, Lady Cattelya Potentilla

Session: Session 17

What Happened

From the grill in a narrow passage behind the East Wing of Potentilla Manor, the party watched Magister Ilex Verbena tell Lady Cattelya Potentilla how the Wilt came up the water stairs. Cattelya sat doodling in her notes, as she often does. Verbena held a grey cloth book with brass corners and red-ruled ledger columns, and her pen wrote in it without her looking.

Calanthe Orchidaceae recognized the book as the same kind Quill, the Crown arcanist, carried at Crown Works Installation Seven: the Grey Ledger-Book used by the Queen's Thorns and Roots. The party now knew Verbena was a Queen's Thorn, not an ordinary official running The Queen's Commission. Cleke, Dez, and Kirill Amaterasu listened at the grill with her. Soon after, a servant summoned them to the orange orchard (Verbena's Offer in the Orange Orchard).

Verbena's Offer in the Orange Orchard

Developing On 2 Caducan 301 Bloom Magister Ilex Verbena names each party member from her grey ledger-book in the Potentilla orchard, says she has reported none of them, and offers safety under the Potentilla roof if they leave Lady Cattelya's sessions alone.

Verbena's Offer in the Orange Orchard

Quick Reference

In-world Date: 2 Caducan 301 Bloom

Era: The Acanthine

Location: Potentilla Manor

Participants: Magister Ilex Verbena, Calanthe Orchidaceae, Dez, Kirill Amaterasu, Cleke

Session: Session 17

What Happened

A servant called the party up to the orange orchard on the fourth terrace of the Potentilla Manor. Magister Ilex Verbena was waiting with her Grey Ledger-Book under her arm. She touched it once for each of them and named them in turn: a monk from the Southern Forests (Kirill Amaterasu), a sorcerer from the roots of Loquat (Dez), an archer who cannot see what he shoots (Cleke), and the daughter of the house (Calanthe Orchidaceae). Their wanted posters were good likenesses, she said, and she had reported none of it to the garrison.

Her offer was plain. Leave Lady Cattelya Potentilla's sessions undisturbed, and the party could sleep safely under the Potentilla roof while the garrison never heard of them. The Queen wants an accurate memorial. Somewhere in the lady's memories is "the one face the Queen needs," a face Cattelya does not know she has seen, and finding it may cost her "a day here, a name there."

Dez took Verbena aside and asked what would stop him from killing Lady Cattelya. She answered that others hold the same knowledge, and that Lord Vanda Potentilla and the household would pay for it while Dez fled. Calanthe did not hear the exchange. Dez backed down. As Verbena walked back toward the house, several of the city-green guards stepped out from among the orange trees and fell in behind her.

Consequences

Kind: other

Allies:

Overview

What this group is and the role it plays in the world. For generations the Lockes shaped Loquat's social order, profiting from The Roots labor and enforcing Canopy supremacy. They framed it as natural order. Vesperae's grandfather was the family's most notorious figure; he described starving children as an inefficient allocation of resources.

Magister Corbin Locke was the first to actively work against that legacy, redirecting resources and building a quiet network of resistances before his assassination in Pollant 301 Bloom. With him gone and Loquat devastated, the family's remaining influence and direction are uncertain. Known members: Magister Corbin Locke (deceased, Pollant 301 Bloom), Vesperae Locke (active, traveling with the party). Vesperae's grandfather, whose name is not recorded, was the defining patriarch of the family's exploitative era.

House Potentilla

Developing Calanthe's adoptive family and stewards of Cinquefoil since before the Empire; coerced into the Queen's Commission under threat of a garrison while Magister Verbena's lessons strip Lady Cattelya's memories in search of someone hidden within the house.

House Potentilla

Quick Reference

Kind: noble house

Leader:

Headquarters: Cinquefoil

Allies:

Enemies:

Overview

What this group is and the role it plays in the world. House Potentilla governs Cinquefoil, Elarosea's city-state of culture and the arts. The family adopted Calanthe Orchidaceae as a young child, recognizing potential they believed worth cultivating. They love her and are protective of her; their reach across the city is wide enough that most people think twice before questioning anything tied to the house. The question of formal succession remains open, with Calanthe the likely but unconfirmed choice. House Potentilla is the Potentilla family manor inside Cinquefoil, seat of Lord Vanda Potentilla and Lady Cattelya Potentilla. The grounds include an east garden wall that Calanthe Orchidaceae used as a childhood entry route before the guard count doubled. Inside the manor are a guest room, Calanthe's preserved bedroom, and a workshop she kept for craft work. By 1 Caducan 301 Bloom the household guard had doubled compared to when Calanthe last lived there, and Alithiel hides somewhere within the estate.

Goals

What they are working toward, openly and in secret.

Structure & Members

How they are organised and who matters.

Assets & Reach

Resources, territory, and how far their influence runs. The house's seat stands at the summit of Cinquefoil, above everything: five terraces of rose-washed walls, green shutters and honey-stone arcades on carved pillars, gardens on every terrace, a courtyard of orange trees round a three-tiered fountain, and a wrought-iron gate that roses have grown through for generations. A garden wall runs along the east side of the grounds. The Queen's commissioners have been lodged in the east wing since the memorial commission began.

Overview

What this group is and the role it plays in the world. The Queen's Roots are Queen Acantha Rosaceae's personal intelligence network. They answer only to the Queen and do not shy away from blackmail, coercion, or manipulation to gather information and maintain her advantage. Known informally as the Roots. Roots agents killed Theron Blackthorn on Crown orders; Lord Corvus Blackthorn signed the warrant. Edmund Vane was a Roots operative before Elara Vane caught him, spared him, and turned him to serve The Hive. (Merged from a duplicate faction note titled The Roots on 2026-09-26.) The Roots are agents of the Crown. The Hive works in direct opposition to the Crown and its agents, but it keeps to the shadows and hides its work from the Crown, so the Roots do not know it exists.

Goals

What they are working toward, openly and in secret.

Structure & Members

How they are organised and who matters.

  • Roots Arcanist Quill (dead), Roots-Agent Nettle, and Magister Ilex Verbena, the lead commissioner in Cinquefoil, posing as the Crown's historian of The Grey Wilt.

Assets & Reach

Resources, territory, and how far their influence runs. They control the deepest vaults beneath Caelrosa, proximity that relates directly to the sealed The Index below the city.

  • 14 Pollant 301 Bloom: A covert Roots base was found hidden inside the Inner Archives of the Grand Archives, with documents on three Crown operations targeting gods in Cinquefoil, Medlar and Blackthorn. (Session 6)

Queen's Thorns

Stub The Queen's agents, Magister Ilex Verbena among them; one of the two sides the party weighs against Sable.

Queen's Thorns

Quick Reference

Kind: military

Leader: Queen Acantha Rosaceae

Headquarters: Caelrosa

Allies:

Enemies:

Overview

What this group is and the role it plays in the world. The Queen's Thorns are Queen Acantha Rosaceae's personal enforcement arm. They answer to no authority but the Queen herself and operate with brutal, unsentimental efficiency. They suppress threats to the Crown directly and without hesitation. The Thorns are soldiers of the Crown. Shortly after Starsfall, a Thorns commander ordered the massacre of the witnesses at Benz and the party was framed for it (see Massacre at Benz). Queen Acantha Rosaceae was in seclusion and gave no order. The commander acted on their own authority, on what they took to be her wishes. The Queen's agents. Grand Magister Magister Ilex Verbena is one of them, and she stood with the Potentillas at the betrothal announcement at Potentilla Manor on 3 Caducan 301 Bloom, the last day of Petal Rest. That afternoon the party weighed siding with the Thorns or with Sable, remembered what their last deal with Sable cost them, and chose to play Aeneas Solanaceae cunningly instead (The Party Spares Aeneas, Session 19).

The Hive

Developing Spy network founded by Elara Vane; true goal is freeing Queen Acantha from the divine power driving her mad, not killing her; allied with Lord Corvus Blackthorn for the Cinquefoil mission

The Hive

Quick Reference

Kind: other

Leader: Elara Vane

Headquarters:

Allies:

Enemies:

Overview

What this group is and the role it plays in the world. An intelligence network built and run by Elara Vane. Known for its reach, its discretion, and its founder's unbroken record of coming out ahead in any deal. Most who interact with the Hive encounter only a fragment of it; the full scope is known to Elara alone. Founded and run by Elara Vane, whose goal is a non-lethal method of separating divine energy from Queen Acantha Rosaceae without killing her. The Hive operates through placed agents; Edmund Vane is the contact stationed in Blackthorn.

Sable defected after disagreeing with the gentle approach, believing the Queen must simply be killed. She now leads her own small faction toward that goal. Both factions share knowledge of Theron Blackthorn's true death. The Hive works in direct opposition to the Crown and its agents, the Queen's Roots. It prefers to work in the shadows and hides its operations from the Crown, so the Roots do not know it exists.

Donation Ledger

Quick Reference

Kind: other

In-world Author: unknown

Given In: Session 12

Document

A ledger recording contributions to a church. Found by Dez in an abandoned prayer shrine in the eastern wing of Crown Works Installation Seven on 24 Pollant 301. No recognized church should exist in Elarosea, where Queen Acantha Rosaceae is the sole sanctioned divine authority. The ledger does not name the deity or congregation. Recovered by Dez from an abandoned prayer shrine off the east corridor of Crown Works Installation Seven. The ledger records donations, suggesting organized worship was conducted there at some point, which sits strangely inside a Crown facility in Elarosea, where no gods besides the Queen are acknowledged.

Grey Ledger-Book

Developing Grey cloth ledger with brass corners and red-ruled columns whose pen writes without the bearer looking; carried by Queen's Thorns and Roots, including Quill and Magister Ilex Verbena.

Grey Ledger-Book

Quick Reference

Category: wondrous

Rarity:

Requires Attunement:

Current Owner: Magister Ilex Verbena

Location: Potentilla Manor

Description

A grey cloth ledger with brass corners and red-ruled columns. The bearer's pen writes in it without the bearer looking at the page. The Queen's Thorns and Roots carry these books; Quill, Crown Arcanist, had one at Crown Works Installation Seven, and Magister Ilex Verbena carries one at Potentilla Manor, where she writes in it during her lessons with Lady Cattelya Potentilla.

On the second day of Petal Rest, in the orange orchard, Verbena touched the book once for each party member and named them from it: Kirill Amaterasu as a monk from the Southern Forests, Dez as a sorcerer from the roots of Loquat, Cleke as an archer who cannot see what he shoots, and Calanthe Orchidaceae as the daughter of the house. Calanthe had already recognized it through the East Wing grill as the same kind of book Quill carried, which is what proved Verbena a Queen's Thorn rather than an ordinary official (Verbena Unmasked as a Queen's Thorn, Verbena's Offer in the Orange Orchard, Session 17). How the book names people on touch is not yet known.

Ledger of Divine Artifacts

Developing Veritas's record of truths; writing something into it makes it real, but because it was designed only for actual truths, contradictions written into it persist as permanent impossibilities

Ledger of Divine Artifacts

Quick Reference

Category: artifact

Rarity: artifact

Requires Attunement: No

Current Owner: Elara Vane

Location: Elara Vane

Description

What it looks like and how it is carried. The Ledger is an authoritative census of divine artifacts, associated with Veritas. It carries a detectable divine signature: the forgery Elara Vane prepared lacked this signature and triggered an alarm in the Grand Archives when the swap occurred on 14 Pollant 301 Bloom. The real Ledger was recovered by the party and delivered to Elara. (Session 5, Session 6, Grand Archives Heist)

Properties

What it does: powers, rules, and attunement. Whatever is written in the Ledger becomes real. Because it was built only to hold truths, writing something that contradicts natural law introduces an impossibility it cannot resolve. When Queen Acantha Rosaceae wrote Elara Aurum's resurrection after the Grey Wilt and never wrote a death for her after, Elara's story entered the record without an ending. It cannot end. See Resurrection of Elara Aurum.

History & Provenance

Who made it, who held it, and how it surfaced.

Connections

  • Veritas
  • Elara Vane
  • Grand Archives
  • Session 5
  • Session 6
  • Grand Archives Heist

The Aether Core

Canon The power source of the Aether Golem, now slotted into the Golem after Sable threw it to Spadae on 25 Pollant 301; both Core and Golem are in Sable's possession.

The Aether Core

Quick Reference

Category: artifact

Rarity: unique

Requires Attunement: No

Current Owner: Sable

Location: The Aether Golem

Description

A purpose-built power source designed to slot into the The Aether Golem and activate it. It was held in the vault beneath Crown Works Installation Seven until 24 Pollant 301, when Sable (then operating as Maret Voss) and Cassimir Blackthorn stole it while the party was occupied in the vault below (see Theft of the Aether Core). The Core also powered the abjuration cage holding Valerius at Crown Works; its removal killed the ward and freed the god. On 25 Pollant 301, Spadae slotted the Core into the Golem at Blackthorn's training grounds, enabling it to consume Valerius.

The Aether Golem

Stub The construct the party lost to Sable along with Spadae in their last deal with her.

The Aether Golem

Quick Reference

Category: artifact

Rarity: unique

Requires Attunement: No

Current Owner:

Location: unknown; left the Blackthorn Training Grounds with Sable's crew on 25 Pollant 301

Description

A construct that Spadae piloted at Blackthorn's training grounds to consume Valerius, under Sable's plan. According to Cassimir Blackthorn, it is now full and cannot take more. Because of that, Sable means to hijack the Crown's operation in Cinquefoil rather than feed the Golem again.

  • 2 Caducan 301 Bloom: At the Cinquefoil Water Stairs, Cassimir tells the party the Golem is already full, so Sable plans to hijack the local Crown operation as she did in Blackthorn (Cassimir Unmasks at the Water Stairs, Session 18).

A construct the party lost to Sable. Spadae piloted it at the training grounds, where it consumed Valerius. When Spadae sided with Sable and left with her, the party lost the Golem too.

  • 3 Caducan 301 Bloom: Weighing Sable against the Queen's Thorns, the party recalled that their last deal with Sable cost them the Golem and Spadae, and chose to play Aeneas cunningly instead (The Party Spares Aeneas, Session 19).

Properties

What it does: powers, rules, and attunement. Two components work in tandem:

  • Golem body (at Blackthorn's central training grounds): draws in and absorbs divine energy from a god.
  • Aether Core (at Crown Works Installation Seven): receives and focuses the absorbed energy into a weaponizable form.

The Crown plans a public demonstration on 26 Pollant 301 using both components together to consume the god in Blackthorn. Sable also intends to consume this god, putting her faction in direct competition with the Crown and on a collision course with the party.

  • The Crown's planned use is detailed in The Aether Golem Demonstration. For what the golem consumes and why that matters, see Nature of Gods in Elarosea.

History & Provenance

Who made it, who held it, and how it surfaced.

Connections

  • Crown Works Installation Seven
  • Blackthorn
  • Sable
  • Three Claims on the Core: the three-faction vault encounter in which the Core was seized and later slotted into the golem.
  • The Aether Golem Demonstration: the public demonstration the golem was built toward, planned for 26 Pollant 301.

Powered by the The Aether Core, which slots into the construct to activate it; Sable and Cassimir Blackthorn took the Core from Crown Works Installation Seven on 24 Pollant 301.

  • Spadae: Hive operative who piloted the Golem on 25 Pollant 301, slotting the Aether Core to consume Valerius at Blackthorn's training grounds.

  • 24 Pollant 301 Bloom: The Aether Core was found in the underground lab at Crown Works Installation Seven, held between energy pylons. Sable's operatives pried it free. See Theft of the Aether Core.

  • 24 Pollant 301: Present in all twelve prisoners recovered from Crown Works Installation Seven. One conditioned prisoner drifted toward a guard's lantern during the escape until Calanthe Orchidaceae intervened. The party found no field cure at the safe house; the prisoners were left behind.

Warm weed numbs the mind and renders those who take it compliant. Val identified it as the drug used on the prisoners held at the safe house after the Crown Works escape. He noted that it is not normally administered to women, making his young sister's dosing anomalous.

Blackthorn

Developing City-state southwest of Cinquefoil where Sable hijacked the Crown operation; home of the Blackthorn lords and Aeneas's deposed family.

Blackthorn

Quick Reference

Kind: city

Region: Elarosea

Ruler / Owner: Lord Corvus Blackthorn

Population:

Factions Present: House Blackthorn

Description

The sights, sounds, and feel on arrival. Blackthorn has walls. The districts in their shadow house soldiers' families and working trades: tanners, weavers, people who stay close to the ground. Val was born here. The city wakes each day to new text written across its walls, always in different locations, always gone by the next morning. The writings are not magical. Cassimir Blackthorn believes they began the day Theron Blackthorn died and are his communications. The central training grounds are the Crown's chosen site for the Aether Golem demonstration on 26 Pollant 301.

The Crown Levy has been active in the city; the families of several residents have been conscripted. Blackthorn is a god-holding city-state under the governance of Lord Corvus Blackthorn. A residual presence of the consumed god Veritas lingered within the city and suppressed the divine being incubating inside Calfur; once the party departed on 25 Pollant 301, that suppression ended and Calfur's condition began to worsen. The party operated from Blackthorn as their base from Session 9 through Session 15, departing by abandoned rail car for the Hive Forest Base northeast toward Cinquefoil. A city and the surrounding territory that bears the Blackthorn name. Castle Blackthorn sits within the city, seat of Lord Corvus Blackthorn and his household. The training grounds on the city's outskirts were the site of the Aether Golem's consumption of Valerius on 25 Pollant 301 (see The Aether Golem Demonstration). Crown Works Installation Seven operated within the city until it burned on 24 Pollant 301. Strange writings have appeared daily on the city's walls since the day Theron Blackthorn died (see Blackthorn Writings). During the current arc, Crown forces, Sable's independent cell, and The Hive all operate within the city simultaneously. A large city and Val's birthplace. Crown Works Installation Seven and a military training grounds both sit within its bounds. After Valerius was consumed by the Aether Golem, a residual presence of Veritas lingered in the city and kept the divine being inside Calfur from worsening. The party departed northeast toward Cinquefoil by abandoned rail car on 25 Pollant 301; Calfur's condition deteriorated as soon as they cleared the city. A city-state southwest of Cinquefoil. It is the seat of Lord Corvus Blackthorn and his son Cassimir Blackthorn. Aeneas Solanaceae's family also comes from Blackthorn: upper class, but deposed. At Blackthorn's training grounds, Sable took over the Crown's operation, and The Aether Golem consumed Valerius. Cassimir says Sable means to do the same thing in Cinquefoil.

Caelrosa

Quick Reference

Kind: city

Ruler / Owner: Queen Acantha Rosaceae

Population:

The sights, sounds, and feel on arrival. Caelrosa is one of Elarosea's city-states. It is the home posting of Layla, a Vice Captain of its guard, who was sent to Benz on a short-staffing detail. Beyond that, its character, governance, and place in Elarosean politics are not yet established. Caelrosa is the site where Queen Acantha Rosaceae descended beneath the earth to confront and consume Veritas, the God of Truth. She made the descent during the Grey Wilt, after the gods refused to answer mortal suffering. The act gave her the power to wage war on the divine and eventually drove her to madness. Caelrosa is the city beneath which Queen Acantha Rosaceae descended to defeat and consume Veritas the God of Truth after the gods ignored her people during The Grey Wilt. The Consumption of Veritas took place here, marking the start of Acantha's war on divinity. Nothing further is known about Caelrosa's current state or its relation to the wider world.

Notable Features

Cinquefoil

Developing God-holding city-state northeast of Blackthorn and Calanthe's adopted home; on the last night of Petal Rest 301 Bloom, green, blue and red fires burn across it.

Cinquefoil

Quick Reference

Kind: city

Region: Elarosea

Ruler / Owner: House Potentilla

Population:

Factions Present:

Description

Cinquefoil is a city-state that holds a god within its borders, making it the next target in Queen Acantha Rosaceae's campaign to harvest divine power. Following the successful extraction at Blackthorn, Acantha's agents moved on Cinquefoil. Elara Vane assigned the party to infiltrate the city, stop the extraction, and arrive before Sable. The Hive Forest Base sits approximately a day's travel northeast. Cinquefoil is also the homeland of House Potentilla, the noble family that adopted Calanthe Orchidaceae. A city-state roughly a day's travel northeast of Blackthorn, past the Hive Forest Base. It holds a god within its bounds, which makes it the current target of Queen Acantha Rosaceae's god-extraction campaign. Calanthe Orchidaceae was adopted into House Potentilla of Cinquefoil and considers it her home.

The city climbs a hill above its river in streets that go up in steps. The walls are washed ochre, terracotta and rose. Every window has green shutters, and every shutter is painted. The balconies are iron, hung with geraniums and washing, and on festival days with paper flowers. The grand buildings are honey-coloured stone: arcades on carved pillars, loggias, courtyards of orange trees round tiered fountains, painted ceilings within. There is no bare stone in Cinquefoil that someone has not tiled, painted or planted over. Cinquefoil is also Elarosea's centre of fashion. The ateliers of the artists' quarter set the cut of each season for Caelrosa, and a Cinquefoil mask or headdress is what the capital wears to a masque. The city keeps Petal Rest (1 Caducan) as a carnival: paper flowers on every wall by day, a parade of dancers, drum lines and feathered headdresses from the gate to the great square by night, and fireworks over the lower town.

Notable Features

Districts, landmarks, and points of interest.

  • A thriving hub of artistic and cultural expression
  • The gate of the forty dancers: the gatehouse is faced in glazed tile, blue, white and gold, showing forty dancers, no two alike. Warrants are hung beside it as paintings.
  • The artists' quarter: print stalls, ateliers, tailors and mask-makers. Charlotte and Charybdis keep a print stall and safe room here for Elara Vane.
  • The great square: site of the The Grey Wilt memorial commissioned by the Queen. A fountain in three tiers under orange trees, and nearby the fountain of the man trying to fix a fountain, which the city loves.
  • House Potentilla at the summit, five terraces above the city.
  • The Academy.
  • The table: Cinquefoil cooks for the eye first. The painted plate, the sugar-modellers, root-red and the name-bread of Petal Rest are in Cinquefoil Cuisine; what the stalls sell is on Cinquefoil Street Food.
  • The city gate posts public warrants; Calanthe Orchidaceae's warrant (treason, sedition, arson) hung there on the opening day of Petal Rest.
  • The Artist Quarter hosts traveling print stalls and merchants during festival season; Charlotte and Charybdis ran a stall there on 1 Caducan 301 Bloom.
  • Petal Rest opens 1 Caducan and fills the city with carnival games, a prediction market, fountain jousting, and a mask tradition at sundown.
  • House Potentilla sits above the city; the manor guard count doubled in the months after Calanthe's disappearance.
  • The city's sleeping patron god Amorphia (beauty, splendor, glory, arts) is dormant within the city and is the target of the Crown's god-consumption plot.

Inhabitants

Who lives here or holds power. House Potentilla governs the city, holding a reach broad enough that most questions about the house go unasked.

Connections

  • Elarosea
  • Session 15

  • Petal Rest

  • The Academy
  • Cinquefoil Cuisine
  • Cinquefoil Street Food
  • House Solanaceae

  • 2 Caducan 301 Bloom: Second day of Petal Rest. A house-high scaffold covered in sailcloth stands in the Great Square for the Wilt memorial, and Magister Ilex Verbena says the unveiling date will be announced the next night. A second masquerade fills the night. At Potentilla Manor, regimented strangers in city green stand guard alongside the house guards, a bearing unlike the city's relaxed watch. Cleke found an identical abjuration aura behind each one's lapel. (Session 17, Party Investigates the Potentilla Manor)

Aeneas had hidden the memorial cutout in the Abandoned Printing Press, in the old scholarly printing district of southeastern Cinquefoil, which emptied after Loquat was founded. That evening, during The Betrothal Announced at Potentilla Manor, green, blue and red fires burned across the city as dozens of green fireballs fell toward Potentilla Manor (Green Fire Falls on Cinquefoil, Session 19).

Crown Works Installation Seven

Developing Crown-staffed facility in Blackthorn holding drugged prisoners and Valerius; burned on 24 Pollant 301; Crown-run, not Blackthorn-operated.

Crown Works Installation Seven

Quick Reference

Kind: building

Region: Blackthorn

Ruler / Owner:

Population:

Factions Present:

Description

The sights, sounds, and feel on arrival. Holds the Aether Core, the part of The Aether Golem that focuses and weaponizes consumed divine energy. The party planned to infiltrate it after scouting the training grounds on 23 Pollant 301. Crown Works Installation Seven is a facility within Blackthorn staffed and operated by the Crown, not by Lord Corvus Blackthorn's household. It held dozens of drugged prisoners, including Val's sister and father, and served as the cage for the war god Valerius before the party freed him during their escape. The facility burned on the night of 24 Pollant 301. Edmund Vane later told Dez that the installation was a Crown operation, leaving it unclear to Lord Corvus how much the party had to do with its destruction. Edmund Vane confirmed to Dez that the facility was staffed and operated by the Crown, not by House Blackthorn. Whether Lord Corvus Blackthorn knew the party burned it down remains unclear.

Notable Features

Districts, landmarks, and points of interest.

  • Eastern wing: An abandoned prayer shrine containing the Donation Ledger, evidence of unsanctioned worship; Dez set a slow fire there on 24 Pollant 301.
  • Western wing: Cells holding prisoners, including Val's family.
  • Central chasm: A large cage hangs over it, which held Valerius until 24 Pollant 301; it now stands open and empty. See Valerius Freed at Crown Works.

A trap door off the main floor leads to a sub-level laboratory: a blood-soaked floor with butchered animals and people, and a set of energy pylons that held the Aether Core until 24 Pollant 301. The installation also holds cell blocks where prisoners are kept in a drugged, glassy-eyed obedience. Val's father and sister were among those held there.

  • 24 Pollant 301: Party infiltrated via the loading carts. Roots Arcanist Quill killed here; see Death of Roots Arcanist Quill. Aether Core stolen by Maret Voss and Cassimir Blackthorn; see Theft of the Aether Core. Val's father and sister freed from the cell blocks; see Val Finds His Family at Crown Works. Party escaped by smashing through a mortared outer wall while the building burned.
  • East corridor shrine: An abandoned prayer room, burned on 24 Pollant 301 after Dez used it as a diversion. The Donation Ledger was recovered here before the fire.
  • Western cell wing: A prison block. Val recognized it as the section holding his family.
  • Central chasm: A large gap crossable only by ceiling beams. A massive cage hangs over it, which held Valerius, the Warmind of strategy and war, until the party freed him on 24 Pollant 301.

A trap door hidden under a chair descends to an underground lab. The space held butchered animals and people, blood across the floor, and the Aether Core locked between energy pylons. The power source feeding the cage's abjuration ward turned out to be the Aether Core itself; when it was pried from its pylons, the ward failed.

  • 24 Pollant 301: The Aether Core was removed from the central vault by Maret Voss's crew. Party fire above triggered the facility alarm; a guard shift rotation was imminent. Roots Arcanist Quill remained at the control wall, slowed and marked. See Session 12, Theft of the Aether Core.

Cell blocks hold rows of prisoners kept in drugged, unresponsive obedience. As of 24 Pollant 301, the main entrance was set ablaze during the party's escape and a section of cell-block wall was breached to provide an exit route. See Val Finds His Family at Crown Works.

Loquat

Developing City whose founding emptied Cinquefoil's scholarly printing district; Dez's home, where his mother lives in the tenements of what used to be Loquat.

Loquat

Quick Reference

Kind: city

Region: Elarosea

Ruler / Owner:

Population:

Factions Present:

Description

A city whose founding emptied the old scholarly printing district of southeastern Cinquefoil, where the Abandoned Printing Press now stands derelict. It is Dez's home. Aeneas Solanaceae mocked him as a parentless slum rat from Loquat and threatened his mother, who lives in the tenements of "what used to be Loquat" alongside a friend, an innkeeper she meets with, and her landlord (Dez Confronts Aeneas at the Printing Press).

Notable Features

Districts, landmarks, and points of interest.

  • Hub of scholars, researchers, and academic institutions

Loquat is sharply divided by elevation and class. The upper tier, the Canopy, houses the wealthy scholastic class (called "Cannies" by those below). The lower tier, the Roots, is a squalid district where the poor and working class live. The gap is social as much as physical: access to education, Academy placement, and resources flows poorly downward. Alicia and Dez both came from the Roots.

  • approx. 17 Pollant 301 Bloom: Loquat fell. Cause unknown. Val and Vesperae arrived as relief assistance and joined the party around 21 Pollant 301 Bloom. See Fall of Loquat.

Loquat is divided between two districts: The Canopy, the elevated tier of spires and hanging gardens where wealthy families and Magisters live, and The Roots, the desert floor below where the working class labors. Artificial streams cascade from Canopy to Roots. Families like House Locke enforced and justified that social order for generations.

In Pollant 301 Bloom, a great beast attacked the city and left it decimated. (See Beast Attack on Loquat.)

  • Pollant 301 Bloom: A great beast attacked and decimated the city. Magister Corbin Locke helped the party defeat it but fell into a coma and was subsequently assassinated. Vesperae Locke returned to find the city destroyed and began helping survivors. (See Beast Attack on Loquat, Assassination of Corbin Locke.)

Potentilla Manor

Developing The Potentilla family seat in Cinquefoil, terraced above the Great Square: breakfast terrace, orange orchard, stripped library, six-table workroom, Vanda's study, and a listening grill behind the East Wing; now patrolled by warded guards in city green.

Potentilla Manor

Quick Reference

Kind: building

Region: Cinquefoil

Ruler / Owner: Lord Vanda Potentilla

Population:

Factions Present: House Potentilla

Description

The Potentilla family seat in Cinquefoil, built in terraces above the Great Square. Breakfast is taken on the second terrace. On the second morning of Petal Rest the house guards there stood facing inward toward the table, and the sailcloth-covered scaffold for the Wilt memorial was visible in the square below. The orange orchard is on the fourth terrace; it was there that Magister Ilex Verbena named the party and made her offer, and where guards in city green stepped out from among the trees to fall in behind her.

Inside are a library, a workroom, Lord Vanda Potentilla's study, guest rooms, and the kitchen. The library has lost its histories of the Wilt and the old nursery songbook that holds the Wilt's lament. Vanda says Verbena borrowed the books for the commission. The workroom holds six tables of covered drafts and a makeshift classroom with a desk and chalkboard, where Verbena gives Lady Cattelya Potentilla her lessons. A grill behind the panelling in a room off the linen passage opens onto a narrow passage behind the East Wing, and anyone standing there can overhear those lessons.

Since the Queen's Commission began, unfamiliar guards in Cinquefoil city green have stood alongside the familiar house guards. They are stiff and regimented, only nod when greeted, and each carries an identical faint abjuration aura from something behind the lapel. The house staff carry no such aura. Late on masque night the workroom door stood open with a lamp burning and the dust sheets pulled back, Cattelya's memorial draft laid across all six tables (Party Investigates the Potentilla Manor, Verbena's Offer in the Orange Orchard, Lord Vanda's Confession in the Study, The Face Cut from the Memorial Draft, Session 17). The manor's Garden Gate opens to the street. House guards carry messages from it, and during Petal Rest Charybdis and Charlotte met the party there dressed as print sellers. The household takes breakfast on the second terrace. Calanthe Orchidaceae keeps an office with a second-floor balcony window, close enough that people outside can overhear what is said inside. Magister Ilex Verbena has her own office in the manor. Aeneas Solanaceae has a room here, where Kirill Amaterasu found the Sealed Letter to Magister Verbena and the Letter from S to Aeneas (Session 18).

Ruler / Owner:

Population:

Description

The Canopy

Developing The elevated wealthy district of Loquat, where prominent families and Magisters live in spires and hanging gardens suspended above the desert

The Canopy

Quick Reference

Kind: other

Region: Loquat

Ruler / Owner:

Population: wealthy elite, Magisters, noble families

Factions Present: House Locke

Description

The sights, sounds, and feel on arrival. The Canopy sits above the desert floor of Loquat, a district of elegant spires, hanging gardens, and artificial streams that cascade down toward The Roots far below. It is beautiful. The wealth that built it came from the labor of those below, and the families who live here have largely preferred not to examine that arrangement closely. House Locke is the family most associated with establishing and enforcing that order across generations.

  • Magister Corbin Locke's covert network operated through Canopy households and institutions. Who its surviving members are is unknown.
  • The Canopy's social structure is bound to institutions that no longer exist in the same form after Loquat's devastation.

Cinquefoil Cuisine

Developing How Cinquefoil eats. Rose-family fruit and bitter orange, the painted plate, the sugar-modellers, root-red, and the name-bread of Petal Rest; a city that cooks for the eye first and names its cooks like painters.

Cinquefoil Cuisine

Quick Reference

Category: culture

Era: current

Related: Cinquefoil, Petal Rest, House Potentilla, Cinquefoil Street Food

Overview

Cinquefoil eats the way it does everything else: for the eye first. The city sits on a hill of orange groves above its river, with goats and sheep on the terraces and thin vines on the south slope, and it cooks from what the rose family gives it, the family that named Elarosea and its cities: quince, medlar, apple, almond, cherry, plum, and the rose itself. Bitter orange sharpens nearly everything. In the capital, Cinquefoil is known for three things at the table: the painted plate, the sugar-modellers, and the name-bread of Petal Rest. In Cinquefoil itself the rule is simpler. A cook is judged on the plate before the fork, and to praise a dish you name the cook.

Details

Staples.

  • Wall-bread. Flat loaves slapped onto the inside wall of a domed oven and peeled off blistered. Torn, never cut, and rubbed with a halved orange and salt while it is too hot to hold.
  • Leaf-cheese. Goat's cheese wrapped in orange leaves and aged in the terrace caves until the leaf's oil is in the rind. The low town eats it young and white. The hill eats it old and grey and pays for the difference.
  • River fish. A small silver fish split down the back and grilled with bitter orange over vine cuttings. Sold on a stick at every landing.
  • Terrace lamb with quince, the autumn dish. The lamb is cooked until it falls and the quince until it holds its shape, which cooks say is the whole trick.
  • Chestnuts, roasted in braziers from the first cold night of Caducan. The sellers' hands are black until spring.
  • Quince-brick. Quince cooked down with sugar until it can be sliced. Eaten with leaf-cheese, carried by carters, and the reason every child in Cinquefoil has a pocket knife.

The painted plate. A Cinquefoil dish is coloured on purpose. Yellow from saffron, red from the cinquefoil root, green from herb oil, the same three colours as the paper flowers on the walls at Petal Rest. A plate that arrives in one colour is a sign of a kitchen that has stopped trying. Cooks are named at the table, and the good ones are known across the city by name the way painters are, and are hired away by the capital the same way.

Sweet. The sugar-modellers work almond paste and spun sugar into flowers, fruit, faces, and, in the week before Petal Rest, masks. Their guild sits in the artists' quarter beside the ateliers and considers itself one of them. Every proper meal ends with something from them that is looked at before it is broken and shared. Lesser sweets: candied orange peel in a twist of paper; rosewater custard; and cinquefoils, the city's namesake, small five-petalled almond pastries sold by the paper cone and eaten five at a time because that is how many petals a cinquefoil has.

Bitter. Root-red is the city's spirit, distilled from the root of the cinquefoil itself, the five-petalled flower the city is named for. The root cuts red and stains what it touches, and the spirit comes out the colour of blood and as bitter as a warrant. It is drunk in one small glass at the end of a meal, and the saying is that it holds the memory of the meal in the mouth. Blossom-water, orange flowers steeped cold, is what everyone drinks from noon on a hot day and what children get for free at every barrel. The south-slope wine is thin, pale and drunk young. On Petal Rest night it is heated with cloves and the last of the year's candied petals and called the last flower.

The table. Dishes enter to a single drum tap and the host names each dish and its cook. It is rude to eat before the plate has been looked at, and rude not to say so. Guests are handed a paper flower at the door and set it on their plate when they are finished, which is how the kitchen knows without anyone asking. The last course is always something to look at rather than to eat, a sugar figure that is broken with the back of a spoon and shared out, and the host chooses it to say something to the guests.

How to eat it. A guest at a Cinquefoil table is taught four things, usually by the lady of the house, and usually while the food goes cold. Look at the plate before you touch it, and say something about it out loud. Tear the wall-bread, never cut it, and rub it with the halved orange and then the salt while it is still too hot to hold. Burnt fingers are expected, and complaining about them is polite. Wait for the host to name the cook before the first bite, because praise goes to the cook by name. When you have had enough, lay your paper flower on the plate. Nobody will ask whether you are finished, and it is rude to make them.

Name-bread. On the eve of Petal Rest every household bakes one loaf, and before it goes into the oven the names of the house's dead are pressed into the crust with the point of a knife. On Petal Rest night the loaf is broken and eaten and nobody speaks the names aloud, because the bread has. Parishes bake a loaf for the dead with no house, its crust pressed with a hundred names, and set it out in the square. Whoever takes a piece reads one name off the crust. This is the nearest thing Cinquefoil has to a prayer.

Who tells it. The sugar-modellers say the painted plate began with them and everyone else copied it. The low town says the plate was painted long before anyone on the hill could afford sugar, and that root-red was a carters' drink before it was a gentleman's. Both are probably right.

Where it came from. The low town's story is the older one, and it goes back to The Grey Wilt, three hundred years ago. The Wilt was a wasting sickness, and the sick would not eat. There was little to give them but bread and an orange. So somebody in the low town painted the plate with what grew there: red from the cinquefoil root, yellow from the orange rind, green from the leaf. The sick looked, and some of them ate. It cured no one, and the low town has never said it did. It says only that nobody was left to look at a bare plate. That is why a Cinquefoil table looks before it eats. The hill learned it afterward and added saffron and sugar, which is the part the sugar-modellers remember. Nobody says who painted the first plate. The low town says "somebody" and leaves it there. The sugar-modellers' guild keeps the oldest painted plate in the city on its hall wall and calls it the first of theirs. It is low-town clay.

The love-apple (proposal, tied to the unrecorded fall of House Solanaceae). The nightshade fruits, love-apples and fire-peppers, came over the border in Solanaceae ships and were the fashion at the capital for a generation. When the house fell, the fruit fell with it, and no hill table in Cinquefoil has served a love-apple since. The low town never stopped eating them, fried in a pan with salt, at stalls nobody from the hill is seen at. A plate of love-apples at a noble table is a small insult or a small kindness depending on who is serving and who is sitting. Aeneas Solanaceae would know which.

In Play

Menus to pull from

Dishes the table can be handed at a word. Each has a name the players can repeat and one line to say about it. Name the cook when you serve it, and let the plate be looked at first.

Breakfast. The manor eats it on the second terrace. The low town eats it standing at the oven door.

  • Wall-bread, hot off the oven wall, with half a bitter orange and a pinch of salt to rub on it. Borage bakes it every morning at the manor.
  • Leaf-cheese: young and white in the low town, old and grey on the hill, and the hill pays for the difference.
  • The painted plate for the bread: a thumb of saffron, a thumb of green herb oil, a thumb of root-red paste. Yellow, green, red.
  • Quince-brick warmed until it runs, spread on the bread.
  • Eggs baked soft in a dish with herb oil and orange rind, green on yellow.
  • Tea on the hill. Blossom-water everywhere else, and free to children at the barrel.
  • A paper flower at every place, to lay on the plate when you are done.

Midday. A board, not a meal. It is brought to wherever you are and laid out to be looked at.

  • The manor's board: goat sausage green with herb oil, candied orange peel in a twist of paper, roasted chestnuts, quince-brick, and a wedge of leaf-cheese.
  • The carter's lunch: quince-brick and leaf-cheese on a board, sliced with your own knife. Two coppers less if you have one.
  • River fish on a stick from any landing stall, split down the back and grilled over vine cuttings with bitter orange.
  • Wall-bread folded round a goat sausage, eaten walking.
  • Chestnut soup with a swirl of orange oil, from the first cold night of Caducan onward.
  • Blossom-water by day. Root-red is for after dark, and anyone who orders it at midday is a carter or a foreigner.

Dinner. Courses enter to a single drum tap, and the host names each one with its cook.

  • First: wall-bread and leaf-cheese with the first cut of the quince-brick, which is the manor's standing opener. Or a painted soup, three colours poured side by side in one bowl, saffron, herb, and root, that nobody stirs until the host has looked at it.
  • Fish: river fish grilled with bitter orange over vine cuttings, or a whole fish baked yellow with saffron and brought in on the leaf it was wrapped in.
  • Meat: terrace lamb with quince, the autumn dish, the lamb cooked until it falls and the quince until it holds. Later in Caducan, kid with bletted medlar. On a plain night, goat sausage in herb oil.
  • Sweet: rosewater custard painted in the three colours; cinquefoils, five to a paper cone; candied petals from the last of the year's flowers.
  • The figure: a sugar piece from the modellers, looked at, then broken with the back of a spoon and shared. The manor's standing order is the fountain of the man trying to fix a fountain. Others to pull from: a sugar mask, the gate of the forty dancers, a quince tree in blossom, a calf.
  • After: root-red, one small glass each, to hold the memory of the meal in the mouth. On Petal Rest night, the last flower instead, the thin south-slope wine heated with cloves and candied petals.

At an inn. The Bitter Orange, the carters' inn at the foot of the water-stairs, serves the carter's lunch, river fish, goat sausage, and root-red by the glass. No painted plate, no drum tap, and the cook is not named because nobody asks. The street stalls of the great square are on Cinquefoil Street Food.

In season in Caducan. Quince all month. Chestnuts from the first cold night. Medlars, bletted on straw, from the third week. The last plums, the first apples, and the first pressing of the south-slope wine. Petal Rest marks the end of the flowering, so after it the table turns from petals to fruit, and the candied petals on a dish are the last of the year.

Cooks to name. Borage, cook at Potentilla Manor, who bakes the wall-bread and is named by Lord Vanda at every course. The goatherds up the hill, who make the leaf-cheese and are praised as "the hill" rather than by name. Pip's father, the baker whose boy rides the square on Petal Rest, who bakes the parish name-bread. The sugar-modellers' guild, named as a guild, whose piece ends every proper meal.

Served so far

  • Dinner at House Potentilla, Session 16. Five courses, entering to the drum tap, each named by Lord Vanda with its cook: wall-bread and leaf-cheese with the first cut of the quince-brick; river fish with bitter orange; terrace lamb with quince; a rosewater custard painted in the three colours; and the sugar figure. This year's figure is the fountain of the man trying to fix a fountain, which the house orders every year because Calanthe loved it as a child. Lady Cattelya says so, and she is right, which is the sting: she remembers that. Root-red after, one glass each. Verbena takes it like someone who has learned to.
  • The carnival. Stalls in the great square are on Cinquefoil Street Food. The parish name-bread sits by the crier's steps.

Althea tells the guests what is on the plate and where it comes from: the bread off the oven wall, the oranges from the house's own trees, the leaf-cheese from the goats up the hill, and the red from the cinquefoil root.
  • Midday at House Potentilla, Session 17 (served 2026-10-04). A board in the dining room with Lord Vanda at table: goat sausage green with herb oil, candied orange peel, and roasted chestnuts, laid out to be looked at. Cleke went through the chestnuts.
  • Anywhere. A Cinquefoil cook by name is a status object. A noble house in the capital that can say who cooks for it has arrived. A kitchen that cannot has not.

Florescence

Developing The recognition Calanthe worked five years toward and was denied; Althea reveals she and Calanthe's parents blocked it to keep her talent from drawing watchers.

Florescence

Quick Reference

Category: culture

Era:

Related: The Academy

Overview

The Florescence is the final requirement for students completing their studies at The Academy. In scope and prestige it functions like a doctoral dissertation: a substantial piece of independent work demonstrating the student has met the standard expected of their family's position. A denied Florescence is quietly scandalous. The student may keep working on it indefinitely, but each additional year invites questions. When the family behind the student has broad reach, those questions tend to go unasked. Florescence is something students work toward at The Academy. Calanthe Orchidaceae made several attempts and her Florescence was denied after five years of work. Its exact nature is not yet established.

  • 3 Caducan 301 Bloom: In Calanthe's office at Potentilla Manor, Aeneas Solanaceae brought up their time at the Academy and her failed Florescence attempts before blackmailing her (Aeneas Blackmails Calanthe, Session 18).

Florescence is a recognition that Calanthe Orchidaceae worked toward for five years and was denied. What it grants and who confers it are not yet recorded.

  • 3 Caducan 301 Bloom: In Calanthe's room at Potentilla Manor, Althea admits that she and Calanthe's parents share the blame for the failed Florescence. Calanthe's talent drew too much attention while people were already watching the family, for the same reason Lady Cattelya Potentilla is sick now, so they got in her way to keep her safe (Session 19, Althea Confesses Her Part in Calanthe's Florescence).

Nature of Gods in Elarosea

Developing Gods are real, present beings that embody a single domain and are incarnate where that domain runs purest; they do not, as a rule, tend their domains

Nature of Gods in Elarosea

Quick Reference

Category: cosmology

Era:

Related: Queen Acantha Rosaceae, Veritas, God of Second Chances, The Hunted God

Overview

Gods in this world are not ideas or distant powers. Each is a real being, the living body of a single domain, present wherever that domain runs and incarnate where it runs purest. A god of plenty exists while a district starves. A god of mending exists while fever empties a quarter and the carts go out full every morning. They are their domains. They do not, as a rule, tend them. This is not a flaw in the divine order by most reckonings. It is simply how gods are. To Acantha, watching people die for want of what a god possessed and spent nothing on, it looked like power held in a closed hand.

Details

A god cannot be consumed by force or surprise. A domain is too large to hold; reaching for it carelessly either scatters the loose essence back into the world or burns the one who grabbed for it to ash. To take a god whole, the consumer must find its locus: the single place where a domain runs so pure that the god is present there as one incarnate thing rather than a diffuse pressure in the air everywhere at once. Outside the locus there is no discrete thing to fight, let alone absorb. At the locus, the god is finite enough to be engaged directly.

Gods do not die, even when defeated and consumed. The divine essence persists inside whoever took it. Queen Acantha Rosaceae is the sharpest illustration of what a god is not. A true god is its domain and feels no strain in being so: it does not hold the domain, it is the domain. Acantha is flesh that holds divine essence the way a clay jar holds a forge-fire. The jar does not become fire. It is under strain every second it is not breaking. This is the line between a god and a being who has consumed one, and it is why a god in its landscape shows no strain while Acantha in hers accumulates it.

In Play

Connections

  • Queen Acantha Rosaceae
  • Veritas
  • God of Second Chances
  • The Hunted God
  • Queen's Divine Backlash

Log

The Bloom

Developing Elarosea's year count, reckoned from the first flowering of the Rose Throne at Queen Acantha's accession

The Bloom

Quick Reference

Category: culture

Era:

Related: The Calendar, Queen Acantha Rosaceae, Consumption of Veritas, The Acanthine, Caelrosa, Veritas

Overview

Elarosea counts its years in Blooms. Each year of Queen Acantha Rosaceae's reign is one flowering of the Rose Throne, and the count runs from the throne's first flowering at her accession. A date is written with the year followed by Bloom: 12 Pollant 301 Bloom. Spoken, it is "the three hundred and first Bloom" or "year three hundred and one of the Bloom." Clerks in Caelrosa use the formal name Acanthine Bloom, which ties the count to its era, The Acanthine.

Year 0 Bloom is the accession, when the calendar reset. Year 1 Bloom is the year of the Consumption of Veritas. There is a quiet ugliness in the image fit for an immortal queen: the throne flowers once a year, every year, with no season in which it rests, and the count of those flowerings is now past three hundred. When Acantha took the Rose Throne, the court recorded that the throne itself came into flower, and the calendar was struck back to Year 0 to mark it. That flowering gave the count its name.

The trouble is that the records which would fix the date belonged to Veritas, god of truths and records, and she consumed him the next year. After Year 1 Bloom there was no longer any independent ledger against which to check Year 0. The reign's own beginning rests on the word of the woman who ate the only witness that could confirm it.

Details

  • Four fixed festivals mark the year: Verdant Oaths (1 Vesthen), Roseil Night (16 Roseil), Starsfall (7 Pollant), and Petal Rest (1 Caducan).
  • The first day of the year, 1 Vesthen, is Verdant Oaths.

In Play

Connections

  • Caelrosa

Demeanour, quirks, and how they treat the party.

Role & Goals

What they do, who they answer to, and what they want. Aeneas Solanaceae comes from the fallen House Solanaceae and has no real standing of his own. He recognized Calanthe Orchidaceae as the likely future ruler of Cinquefoil and inserted himself into her life deliberately, arriving as a sympathetic confidant during her years of academic difficulty. He keeps himself emotionally present and useful. His aim is to be close enough to power that when it arrives, he arrives with it. His relationship with Calanthe Orchidaceae is built on calculation, not affection. He chose her because of who she will likely become and has maintained the appearance of genuine care ever since. He is aware of the gap between how she sees him and what he actually wants.

Edmund Vane

Developing Elara Vane's trusted second in command; originally a Queen's Roots spy who was caught by Elara, spared, and turned; no familial connection to Elara Vane

Edmund Vane

Quick Reference

Pronouns: he/him

Race / Ancestry: unknown

Role / Occupation: Hive operative

Faction: The Hive

Location: Blackthorn

Disposition: friendly

Appearance

What they look like at a glance, and the one detail that sticks.

Personality

Demeanour, quirks, and how they treat the party.

Role & Goals

What they do, who they answer to, and what they want. Made contact with the party on the morning of 23 Pollant 301. He explained that the Crown's Aether Golem demonstration on 26 Pollant 301 is designed to let the Crown consume the god in Blackthorn and weaponize that divine power. He tasked the party with investigating and stopping it.

History

Where they came from and how they ended up here. Edmund was placed inside the Hive by the Queen's Roots to spy on Elara Vane's operation and feed the Crown intelligence on its activities. During his time undercover, Elara saved his life. That act forged a genuine bond between them. When she eventually discovered his true role, she chose not to have him killed. Instead she offered him a clean break from the Roots and, in time, a position at her right hand. He now serves as her most trusted operative and second in command. The shared surname with Elara is coincidental, not the result of any familial connection.

Elara Vane

Elara Vane

Faction: The Hive

Location:

Personality

Demeanour, quirks, and how they treat the party.

Role & Goals

What they do, who they answer to, and what they want.

History

Born as Elara Aurum, she died of The Grey Wilt centuries ago. Queen Acantha Rosaceae used Veritas's Ledger to write her back from death (Resurrection of Elara Aurum), and the act left her immortal. She founded The Hive on the conviction that Acantha deserves to be freed from the divine madness the consumed power caused, not killed. The ledger Acantha used was later stolen by the party during the Grand Archives Heist.

She defected because of Elara's insistence on a non-lethal approach. The false identities Elara provided the party were discarded on their arrival in Blackthorn.

  • 25 Pollant 301: In the forest base planning room, revealed her true identity as Elara Aurum; disclosed she died of The Grey Wilt centuries ago and was written back to life and left immortal by Queen Acantha Rosaceae using Veritas's ledger, the same ledger the party recovered in the Grand Archives Heist (Resurrection of Elara Aurum); named The Hive's true goal as freeing Acantha from the stolen divine power driving her mad rather than killing her; assigned the party to halt the god-extraction at Cinquefoil before Sable arrives (Elara Reveals Her History to the Party).

Magister Corbin Locke

Developing Magister of Loquat from House Locke who broke from his family's exploitative legacy to build a covert reform network; burned his remaining life force to help defeat the beast at Loquat and was assassinated in his coma on 19 Pollant 301

Magister Corbin Locke

Quick Reference

Pronouns: he/him

Race / Ancestry:

Role / Occupation: Grand Archivist of Loquat, Magister of House Locke

Faction: House Locke

Location: Loquat

Disposition: ally

Appearance

What they look like at a glance, and the one detail that sticks.

Personality

Demeanour, quirks, and how they treat the party.

Role & Goals

What they do, who they answer to, and what they want. Corbin occupied a Magister position in Loquat while quietly redirecting resources, protecting individuals, and building a network of small resistances under the cover of institutional standing and his family name. He did not seek open confrontation with House Locke's legacy but worked to hollow out its worst effects from within. He made compromises and accepted what he judged as acceptable losses; the full extent of those calculations is something his daughter Vesperae Locke does not know. Born into House Locke, Corbin was raised in the environment his family had built: The Canopy's stratified wealth sustained by The Roots below. His own father was openly callous about poverty in the Roots, treating it as a resource allocation problem. Corbin broke from that, beginning a slow private program of reform. He raised Vesperae Locke watching him do this, shielding her from the harder calculations behind his choices.

In Pollant 301 Bloom, a great beast attacked Loquat. Corbin used his resources or abilities to help the party defeat it at significant personal cost and fell into a coma. He was assassinated while unconscious, before Vesperae could reach him. The full scope of his covert resistance network: who its members are, how it was funded, whether it survives him. The specific compromises and acceptable losses he made to hold his position. Whether any of his choices directly harmed people he claimed to protect.

  • Pollant 301 Bloom: Helped the party defeat the beast that attacked Loquat at the cost of his own health; fell into a coma. Assassinated before Vesperae Locke could reach him. (See Beast Attack on Loquat, Assassination of Corbin Locke.)

Gave Calanthe Orchidaceae a cryptic non-answer on killing Queen Acantha Rosaceae, saying only that the line between naivety and hopelessness is almost invisible. See Party Visits the Dreamcatcher.

Queen Acantha Rosaceae

Developing The reigning queen, driven mad by divine power she consumed from Veritas; the Hive's true goal is to free rather than kill her.

Queen Acantha Rosaceae

Quick Reference

Pronouns: she/her

Race / Ancestry:

Role / Occupation: ruler

Faction:

Location: Caelrosa

Disposition:

Appearance

What they look like at a glance, and the one detail that sticks.

Personality

Demeanour, quirks, and how they treat the party.

Role & Goals

What they do, who they answer to, and what they want.

  • Maintains two private forces answering to her alone: the Queen's Thorns (enforcement) and the Queen's Roots (intelligence).

Acantha is not a god. She is a mortal body holding one, which is not the same thing. A true god is its domain and feels no strain in being so; Acantha is flesh that was never meant to hold divine essence and now does. The divine within her does not age, does not tire, does not let the body around it fail, so the body does not. Cut her and she heals. Wait her out and you will die first. What people call her immortality sits closer to demigod than to goddess: a mortal lit from inside by something that will not burn down.

The cost is in that holding. One domain in mortal flesh strains, and she long ago stopped being able to live on Veritas alone, so she takes more, each new god a fresh coal banked against the day the first burns low. The domains do not agree inside her. Truth and each subsequent god grind where their natures meet, and that grinding has a name in the documents circulating among those who study her (see Queen's Divine Backlash). The thing that keeps her from dying is the same thing slowly tearing her apart.

  • What she carries is shaped by Nature of Gods in Elarosea: gods incarnate where their domain runs purest, and she holds that essence within a mortal frame.

History

Where they came from and how they ended up here.

  • Her immortality came from consuming Veritas, god of Truths and Records (Year 1). The calendar was reset to Year 0 at the start of her reign, with Year 1 marking the year of the consumption. The exact mechanism is unknown.
  • 24 Pollant 301 Bloom: Nal'Szomn confirmed she consumed the God of Death. Sable confirmed under Zone of Truth she killed Theron Blackthorn. The Index is identified as the site where she first sealed a god into herself. See Party Visits the Dreamcatcher and Sable Questioned Under Zone of Truth.
  • 24 Pollant 301 Bloom: Confirmed by Nal'Szomn to have consumed the God of Death, making resurrection of the dead impossible for anyone outside her power. See Party Visits the Dreamcatcher.
  • The Grey Wilt (ancient era): The gods ignored the dying population during the plague. Acantha descended beneath Caelrosa, defeated Veritas the God of Truth, and consumed his power (Consumption of Veritas). She then waged war on every god that had failed her people, but the stolen divine power drove her mad before she could finish.
  • Centuries ago: Used Veritas's ledger to write Elara Vane (then Elara Aurum) back from death, leaving her permanently immortal as a consequence (Resurrection of Elara Aurum). The same ledger was later stolen by the party in the Grand Archives Heist (Veritas's Ledger).
  • ~296 Bloom: Entered seclusion and dispatched agents to god-holding city-states to extract their resident gods. The operation in Blackthorn succeeded when the Aether Golem consumed Valerius; Cinquefoil is the next target. (Session 15)

Connections

  • Caelrosa
  • Elarosea
  • The Bloom
  • Session 15
  • Elara Reveals Her History to the Party

Log

  • 14 Pollant 301 Bloom: Party discovered Crown documents revealing three active operations developing autonomous god-consumption methods: Surveillance: Alithiel, Surveillance: Aeolus, Surveillance: Valerius. The Queen's seclusion and the true reason behind these experiments remain a closely held secret. (Session 6, Queen's Divine Backlash)
  • 1 Bloom: Consumed Veritas at The Index after a long direct fight. See Consumption of Veritas.

Description

Acantha was a ruling queen during The Grey Wilt, a time when the gods gave no aid to the dying. She went beneath Caelrosa, defeated and consumed Veritas the God of Truth (see Consumption of Veritas), then turned that stolen power against every god that had failed her people. Before that war, she used Veritas's ledger to write Elara Vane back to life after Elara died of the plague, leaving her immortal (see Resurrection of Elara Aurum). The stolen divine power eventually drove her mad. Approximately five years before 301 Bloom, she withdrew into seclusion and dispatched agents to god-holding city-states to extract their divinities. Those agents succeeded in Blackthorn on 25 Pollant 301; Cinquefoil is next. The Hive exists to free her from the divine power destroying her sanity, not to kill her. The reigning queen of the realm. She absorbed a divine power at some point in the distant past; the madness it causes has shaped her rule ever since. Centuries ago she resurrected Elara Vane (then dying of the Grey Wilt) using Veritas's ledger, leaving Elara permanently immortal. The Hive's publicly stated goal of killing the queen is a cover; its actual aim is to free Acantha from the divine power consuming her mind. The party's shared vision at Benz named "she" as Acantha, warning them to find the unconsumed gods before she does (see Find the Others).

Sable

Developing Defected Hive operative whose Golem plan consumed Valerius in Blackthorn; now in Cinquefoil hunting what the Crown seeks, planning to hijack the local operation, and recruiting Aeneas Solanaceae as an informant.

Sable

Quick Reference

Pronouns: she/her

Race / Ancestry: unknown

Role / Occupation: Splinter faction leader

Faction: The Hive

Location: Blackthorn

Disposition: hostile

Appearance

What they look like at a glance, and the one detail that sticks.

Personality

Demeanour, quirks, and how they treat the party.

Role & Goals

What they do, who they answer to, and what they want. Sable split from Elara Vane's Hive because she believes Queen Acantha Rosaceae must be killed outright, not gently divested of her divine power. She leads a small faction committed to that goal.

In Blackthorn she travels as Maret Voss, a former infantry sergeant who served alongside Theron Blackthorn in his final campaign. The cover gives her a credible reason to approach Cassimir Blackthorn, whom she believes is the key to reaching the god inside the city. Her timeline is tight: the Crown's Aether Golem demonstration on 26 Pollant 301 will absorb that god first if she does not act sooner.

She and the Hive both know Theron was killed by the Queen's Roots, not in honourable battle. She has told Cassimir a version of the betrayal (the Crown's hand, the warrant his father signed) and paired it with a promise she knows is false: that by harnessing the god in Blackthorn she can bring Theron back. Made contact with Cassimir on 23 Pollant 301 and arranged a midnight meeting for 24 Pollant 301.

  • Now operates with Spadae and Cassimir Blackthorn from an undisclosed base following Valerius's consumption.

Concept

Before joining the party, Spadae was forced to execute his cousin Icarly. Theron Blackthorn

Developing Elder son of Lord Corvus Blackthorn, killed by Roots agents on Crown orders while the official account says he died with honour; Sable dangles his return to steer Cassimir, and the Blackthorn Writings that began the day he died remain unexplained.

Theron Blackthorn

Quick Reference

Pronouns: he/him

Race / Ancestry: unknown

Role / Occupation: Deceased heir of Blackthorn

Faction: House Blackthorn

Location:

Disposition: unknown

Appearance

What they look like at a glance, and the one detail that sticks.

Personality

Demeanour, quirks, and how they treat the party.

Role & Goals

What they do, who they answer to, and what they want.

History

Where they came from and how they ended up here. Theron was the favoured elder son and heir of Lord Corvus Blackthorn. He served in a military campaign, and the official account has him dying with honour. The truth, held by Elara Vane's Hive and by Sable, is that he was backstabbed by agents of Queen Acantha Rosaceae's Roots on the orders of the Crown. Until 24 Pollant 301 nobody outside the Hive knew this; Cassimir now holds Sable's version of it (the warrant, the Crown's hand).

Cassimir Blackthorn believes the Blackthorn Writings began the day Theron died and are his communications from beyond death. Sable uses her Maret Voss persona to exploit that grief: as of 24 Pollant 301 she has fed him the betrayal (the warrant his father signed) and paired it with a promise she knows is false, that harnessing the god in Blackthorn can bring Theron back.

  • 24 Pollant 301 Bloom: Sable confirmed under Zone of Truth that Queen Acantha Rosaceae killed him and that Lord Corvus Blackthorn's inaction let it happen. See Sable Questioned Under Zone of Truth and Theron's True Death.

Log

Description

An unnamed woman living in the Blackthorn slums whose sanity had broken before Val's return. When Val carried Val's Sister and Val's Father back to her on 25 Pollant 301, the sight of them restored her. The family shared a last meal before Val left to continue the mission. She pressed a parting gift of ten gold on Val, who refused, then let her keep it after he persuaded her to. She directed the party to a support group for conscription victims, founded by the late Lady of Blackthorn, which took in the dozen captives freed from Crown Works Installation Seven. Vesperae Locke used twilight magic to leave her with warmth before the party departed. Lives in the Blackthorn slums. Her sanity had deteriorated before Val's return, though the cause is not stated. When Val carried his sister and father home, her clarity returned and the family shared a last meal before he rejoined the mission. Vesperae Locke used twilight magic to leave her with warmth before the party departed. She knew of the conscription-victim support group founded by the late Lady of Blackthorn and directed the party to it. She offered Val ten gold as a parting gift; he refused, then persuaded her to keep it.

Operations

Stop the Cinquefoil God-Extraction

Developing Elara's assignment to the party: reach Cinquefoil before Sable and stop Acantha's agents from extracting the city's god.

Stop the Cinquefoil God-Extraction

Quick Reference

Status: active

Mastermind: Elara Vane

Target: Cinquefoil

Location: Cinquefoil

Overview

Elara revealed in the Hive Forest Base planning room that Queen Acantha Rosaceae has been sending agents into god-holding city-states since she entered seclusion five years ago. Blackthorn is done; Cinquefoil is next. Elara assigns the party to infiltrate the city, locate and stop the extraction apparatus before it fires, and arrive ahead of Sable. Lord Corvus Blackthorn joins the effort, turned against Sable by Cassimir Blackthorn's defection to her side, along with his white-clad barbarian aid Kefra Chico. Assignment given in Session 15.
